# Issues register

Rules in `04-handoff-and-validation.md` §4. One row per issue; never delete rows — change `status`.
IDs are sequential `ISS-nnn`. Severity: `S1` breaks demo / hard rule / private data / red check;
`S2` wrong or missing behaviour the storyboard needs; `S3` cosmetic or polish; `S4` tech debt,
assumption marker, deferral.

| ISS-077 | 2026-09-13 | T-003 | validator | S2 | contracts/ids | `newPublicId()` delivers 46 bits of entropy, not the 50 its comment claims, and every public id ends in `4` or `m` | `packages/contracts/src/ids.ts:36-45` — `uuidV4().replace(/-/g,'').slice(0, 13)` takes 13 hex chars, but hex index 12 is UUIDv4's version nibble, always `'4'`. It occupies the low 4 bits of `n`, and since `out` is built by prepending, the `i = 0` symbol (`n & 31`) is the **last** character: its alphabet index is always `0b?0100`, i.e. 4 (`'4'`) or 20 (`'m'`). Measured over 50 000 generated ids: positions 0-8 each use all 32 symbols, position 9 uses exactly 2. The shipped `ids.test.ts` uniqueness property still holds (50 000/50 000 unique), which is why it does not catch this. Corrects the "repacks 50 of its bits" claim in `ISS-073`. See `docs/validation/T-003.md` | fixed in CR-002 — `ids.ts` now slices hex chars 19-31 (no version/variant nibble); regression test added to `ids.test.ts` asserting every one of the 10 positions reaches more than 2 distinct symbols over 20 000 ids | T-003 (re-work) |

| ISS-112 | 2026-09-13 | T-004 | validator | S2 | database/auth | The `modelName`/`fields` mapping block T-004 wrote for T-101 to pass to Better Auth's Drizzle adapter is wrong on both halves, and is annotated as "Verified against Better Auth's 1.7.x Drizzle-adapter docs" — following it verbatim makes Better Auth throw at init | `packages/database/src/schema/auth.ts:10-38`. Checked against Better Auth **v1.7.4** tagged source (master plan §3 pins 1.7.3): `packages/drizzle-adapter/src/drizzle-adapter.ts` resolves a model as `schema[modelName]` — the **JS export key** of the Drizzle schema object, not the SQL table name — and throws `The model "x" was not found in the schema object`; this package exports `authUsers`, not `auth_users`, so `modelName: 'auth_users'` will not resolve. Columns are resolved as `schemaModel[getFieldName(...)]` — the **Drizzle property key**, not the SQL column name — so `fields: { emailVerified: 'email_verified' }` makes the adapter look for a property literally named `email_verified` and fail. The table columns themselves are correct: camelCase property + snake_case column name (`emailVerified: boolean('email_verified')`) is exactly what `npx auth generate` emits and needs **no `fields` block at all**. `usePlural` is not a fix either (it appends a literal `"s"` → `auth_userss`), and there is no global casing option for the Drizzle adapter (`casing: 'snake'\|'camel'` belongs to the built-in Kysely config). Same file's column *set* is right: all four tables match 1.7.x exactly, and `account.issuer` is correctly absent (required only in 1.7.0–1.7.2, removed in 1.7.3) | open — correct the comment block to: no `fields` block, no `usePlural`, and map model names either by aliasing the schema object passed to the adapter (`{ ...schema, auth_users: schema.authUsers, … }`) or by renaming the exports. T-101 must not take the current block at face value; fixed in U8 T-101 — `apps/web/src/server/better-auth/instance.ts` maps model names only, with no `fields` block, and the `schema/auth.ts` comment says so (BR7.3; its stale path is ISS-446) | T-101 (due Phase 1 gate) |
| ISS-113 | 2026-09-13 | T-004 | validator | S4 | database/auth | Two smaller Better Auth fidelity gaps in `schema/auth.ts`: the three indexes Better Auth 1.7's generator emits are missing, and `auth_users.name` is nullable where Better Auth declares it required | `packages/database/src/schema/auth.ts`. (1) Better Auth 1.7 emits `session_userId_idx`, `account_userId_idx` and `verification_identifier_idx`; `select … from pg_indexes where tablename like 'auth_%'` against the migrated database returns only the 4 primary keys plus `auth_users_email_unique` and `auth_sessions_token_unique` — so `auth_sessions.user_id`, `auth_accounts.user_id` and `auth_verifications.identifier` are unindexed, and every "list my sessions" (`/me/security`) and revoke-all-sessions lookup seq-scans. (2) `name: text('name')` is nullable; Better Auth's core schema has `name` `required: true`. Harmless in practice — 1.7's runtime schema check only reports missing tables, missing columns and *unexpected* NOT NULL columns, so a nullable required column passes and Better Auth always supplies a value — and data-model §1 says `name` is unused anyway; recorded so T-101 does not re-derive it | fixed in U8 T-101 — migration `0009_auth_indexes_and_email_dedupe.sql` adds the three indexes; `name` stays nullable | T-101 |

Runs 1 and 3–8 were green (one of them from a manual destructive `DROP SCHEMA public CASCADE`), so the reset/migration path itself is idempotent — the hang is lock contention: a `pg_stat_activity` sample during a healthy run caught a backend `idle in transaction` holding `CREATE TABLE "audit_events"`, and `DROP SCHEMA public CASCADE` queues behind such a backend indefinitely. Vitest isolates module state per test file, so `test-setup.ts`'s `reset` promise runs a **full drop + 6-migration cycle per file**; T-005 took the project from 2 collected files to 6 (7 with this validation's probe file), tripling the exposure | fixed in CR-007 — the reset runs once per run in a Vitest `globalSetup` (`packages/database/src/global-setup.ts`); `reset.ts` runs the drop with `lock_timeout = 10s` (sessions opened from the reset URL, migrate included) and `statement_timeout = 60s`, and a blocked drop now fails naming the backend that holds the lock; a guarded sweep in the global setup ends stale backends of the connecting role on `_test`/`_test_b` databases only. Evidence (U2 CR-007): before the fix a held `idle in transaction` blocker hung `pnpm test:int` until the outer 120 s cut-off, with two `DROP SCHEMA` backends still queued; after the fix the same blocker fails the run in 13 s wall (`blocked by backend pid 22001 (role postgres, application "cr007-blocker" …)`), and an `rk_app` blocker is swept and the run is green. 10 consecutive runs green: 30.04, 30.28, 28.25, 30.18, 30.00, 30.00 (from a manual `DROP SCHEMA public CASCADE`), 30.24, 30.02, 30.17, 30.06 s; median 30.05 s, max 30.28 s. | ISS-452 | 2026-11-04 | U8 T-101 | reviewer | S1 | application/database | The web pool could run out and hang the process: every command ran its handler inside an open `UnitOfWork` transaction, and sign-in, register, verify, reset, change password, resend and sign-out called Better Auth from inside it. The library and its session hook take more connections from the same pool (`new Pool` with the node-postgres defaults: 10 connections, no acquire timeout), so about 10 parallel calls, for example correct sign-ins, which have no limit, each held one connection and waited for another for ever, and `/api/health` queued behind them (review R-01; design § 13 Q4 puts the library call first and the application transaction after it) | Tests `packages/application/src/modules/account/commands/library-first.test.ts` (no transaction is open at any gateway call), `apps/web/src/server/auth.int.test.ts` "completes 15 parallel registrations and 15 parallel successful sign-ins on the 10-connection pool", `packages/database/src/client.int.test.ts` "fails a query fast when every connection is taken". Fixed in U8 T-101: `defineCommand` gains library-first commands (`libraryFirst: true`), whose handlers read through `unitOfWork.read`, call the library with no transaction open, then write their rows in a short `unitOfWork.run`; register deletes the library user when that transaction fails, and verify, reset and change password retry it up to 3 times and log. `createDb` sets `max: 10`, `connectionTimeoutMillis: 5000` and `idleTimeoutMillis: 30000` (`DEFAULT_POOL_OPTIONS`), so a full pool fails the request as `internal` instead of hanging. The second connection of ISS-403 goes with it | fixed in U8 T-101 | — |
| ISS-453 | 2026-11-04 | U8 T-101 | reviewer | S2 | auth/limits | Parallel attempts got past the sign-in and change-password limits: `account.signIn` read the stored failures, ran the Argon2 check (about 91 ms) and only then recorded the failure, so a burst of wrong passwords for one address all read the same count and passed the 10-per-email and 30-per-network limits and the progressive delay (BR3.2, BR3.3, C5), and could skip the severe-activity notice. `account.changePassword` had the same read-then-record pattern for its 5 failures per 15 minutes (BR5.3) (review R-02) | Tests in `packages/application/src/modules/account/commands/sign-in.test.ts` "account.signIn limits under parallel attempts" (15 parallel wrong passwords let only `delayAfterFailures` through, 40 from one network let 30 through, refused attempts record nothing) and `change-password.test.ts` "lets at most five of 15 parallel wrong passwords past the check". Fixed in U8 T-101: the `RateLimiter` port replaces `failures` and `recordFailure` with one atomic `reserveFailure(key, windowSec)`, which counts the attempt and returns the failures counted before it; a refused or successful attempt releases its reservation, a failed one keeps it as the recorded failure. ASSUMPTION(T-101): attempts still in flight count against the limits and the delay, so parallel correct sign-ins for one address beyond the delay threshold answer `auth.rate_limited` until the others finish | fixed in U8 T-101 | — |
